Chapter 976: Why Is It So Dark?

“At last, Intermediate Divine Transfiguration Stage,” Lu Yang exclaimed in the pitch-black cave abode.

He opened his eyes, their gleaming brightness standing out starkly in the darkness.

“Wait, why is it so dark?”

“I felt that the candle would ruin the atmosphere, so I blew it out,” the Everlasting Immortal Fairy explained.

Since solar lamps were now widespread, few people still used candles. Lu Yang’s cave abode used merfolk candles, which had yet to need replacement.

Lu Yang shook his head in resignation. At my current cultivation level, it doesn’t matter whether candles are lit; I can see clearly regardless. If the Everlasting Immortal Fairy enjoys blowing them out, then let her be.

He stretched lazily. To break through to the Intermediate Divine Transfiguration Stage, he had gone into seclusion for a whole six hours. Now that his seclusion had concluded, he felt much more comfortable.

Then, he sat back down.

“Let me consolidate my cultivation realm for a bit.”

His foundation was solid, so only a little consolidation was needed.

Once his cultivation realm had stabilized, Lu Yang pushed open the cave abode’s door to find Yun Mengmeng seated with her back to him, facing a chessboard in deep contemplation. She picked up a black piece. With a clack, she placed it down, signaling the game’s conclusion.

“Five in a row; I win!” Yun Mengmeng cheered.

“Sister Mengmeng, Third Senior Sister, can you find somewhere else to play?” Lu Yang said helplessly. Do you have to play Five in a Row right outside my door? You weren’t here when I started my seclusion. When did you arrive?

Gan Tian explained with a smile, “Mengmeng said this spot has good feng shui. By staying close to your cave abode, she can absorb your luck and boost her chances of winning.”

“How is my luck any good?” Lu Yang was perplexed. Is she referring to being latched onto by the Everlasting Immortal Fairy?

“Little Zhi said that half-immortals and immortals aren’t as common in the outside world as I thought. It’s due to your good luck that you keep encountering them.

“See? I told you your luck is good.” Yun Mengmeng pointed triumphantly at the chessboard. “By staying near your cave abode and absorbing your fortune, I won the first game.”

Beside the chessboard lay Osmanthus-filled biscuits; the winner of every round would eat one.

Based on what Lu Yang knew of Yun Mengmeng’s chess skill, he suspected Gan Tian was letting her win.

Having just broken through, Lu Yang was in high spirits. He decided to descend the mountain to see how Meng Jingzhou and the others were doing.

Yun Mengmeng called out to Lu Yang, still chewing a biscuit. “Oh, right, Little Zhi said she’s out visiting friends and probably won’t return today. Second-in-Command, since you’ve broken through, how about the three of us go to the Hundred Fragrance Restaurant tonight to celebrate?”

“Sure.” Lu Yang smiled. Breaking through to the Intermediate Divine Transfiguration Stage is indeed worth celebrating.

After he walked away, the two women began their second game.

“By the way, Mengmeng, why do you keep calling Little Junior Brother ‘Second-in-Command?’” Gan Tian was baffled. Did Little Junior Brother and Mengmeng swear brotherhood at some point?

Yun Mengmeng revealed a mysterious smile. “Secret.”

Lu Yang visited Meng Jingzhou’s cave abode, but unfortunately, he was in seclusion.

Man Gu had been invited to serve as a judge for a Nascent Soul-level competition held in some province. As a Divine Transfiguration cultivator of the Dao-Seeking Sect, his status carried immense weight in the outside world, practically elevating him to the ranks of high-level cultivators.

“Junior Brother Li, what are you busy with... Senior Su, you’re here too? Greetings, Senior Su.” Lu Yang hadn’t expected to find Su Yiren here.

“Mmhm, I calculated that Haoran should be breaking through to the Intermediate Divine Transfiguration Stage, so I came to guard over his cultivation.” Su Yiren smiled sweetly as she peeled an apple for Li Haoran, cutting it into small, bite-sized pieces.

“Is there even a need for a protector when breaking through to the Intermediate Divine Transfiguration Stage?” Li Haoran mumbled, looking down. Breaking through was a natural process and would likely take less than half a day. However, Su Yiren had gone out of her way to come, which made him embarrassed, especially when it was pointed out in front of a friend.

Unlike other Divine Transfiguration cultivators who busied themselves with various preparations before a breakthrough, he was on the verge of breaking through yet still had leisure to design artifacts and magical treasures; he didn’t need to prepare anything.

Or rather, he had already prepared the most crucial element—his mental state.

Li Haoran’s blueprints covered the entire cave abode, underfoot and overhead; all depicted a single magical treasure.

Lu Yang knew little about artifact refinement. He tilted his head, studying Li Haoran’s blueprints for a while without figuring out what he intended to create. He could only discern a sword array from the diagrams.

“What are you drawing here?”

“An automatic wok. This is the water sphere array; this is the sword array; and this is the rootless fire array. As long as the raw ingredients are prepared, this artifact can automatically wash, chop, and cook. The flavor should be comparable to that of a basic chef.”

“Good stuff!” Lu Yang praised it fervently. If such an artifact can be forged and then upgraded further, wouldn’t it completely block the Everlasting Immortal Fairy’s culinary path?

“No magical artifact can ever replace this immortal’s cooking.” The Everlasting Immortal Fairy was dismissive of Li Haoran’s creation.

“Right? Senior Brother Lu, you agree it’s good stuff too.” Li Haoran trusted Lu Yang’s judgment.

Seeing that Li Haoran was on the verge of a breakthrough, Lu Yang refrained from deliberately flaunting his cultivation, lest it create a crack in Li Haoran’s mental state during his breakthrough.

“I’ll treat you to a meal after you’ve broken through,” Lu Yang said, then bid goodbye to Li Haoran and Su Yiren.

On his way to find Tao Yaoye, he bumped into several disciples from the latest cohort, who respectfully addressed him as “senior brother.” Their entrance trials, overseen by Dai Bufan, had gone smoothly, with no unexpected incidents.

“What a pity. When they were participating in the entrance trials, Old Meng and I were in the Demon Kingdom. We didn’t get to help Senior Brother Dai share the burden.”

“Junior Sister Tao, are you there?” Lu Yang activated the array at the entrance of Tao Yaoye’s cave abode, which would alert its occupant.

Tao Yaoye peeked her head out. Seeing only Lu Yang, she smiled and pulled him inside. “Senior Brother Lu, please come in.”

Inside, Lu Yang saw a small mountain of spirit stones, all high-grade; there was not even a single low-grade or mid-grade one in sight.

“Are all these yours?”

“These are just this quarter’s earnings from the Dreamlike Bubble Mirage. They were just delivered, and I’m still counting.”

Lu Yang inwardly marveled. With the number of spirit stones Junior Sister Tao possesses now, she might have plenty left even after cultivating to the Transcendent Tribulation Stage.

“Rich woman. If your senior brother ever runs out of funds, I’ll need your support, Junior Sister Tao.”

“What are you saying, Senior Brother? If you need spirit stones, you can have all these here.”

“How could I do that?” Lu Yang quickly waved his hands. He was only joking.

After chatting with Tao Yaoye for a while, he noticed that it was getting late. As promised, Lu Yang headed to the Hundred Fragrance Restaurant, where Yun Mengmeng and Gan Tian were waiting for him in a private room.

“Come, come, come. Let’s celebrate the Second-in-Command’s breakthrough!” Yun Mengmeng raised her cup, filled with freshly squeezed peach juice.

Gan Tian’s cup held spring water.

Lu Yang’s cup contained osmanthus tea.

Although it was supposed to be a celebration for Lu Yang, it was really just Yun Mengmeng satisfying her cravings. Eating alone was no fun for her, and she wanted company. Food simply tasted better with more people.

After the three finished their meal, Lu Yang thanked Yun Mengmeng for her treat and returned to his cave abode, sitting cross-legged to cultivate for the night.

“Finally, Intermediate Divine Transfiguration Stage,” Lu Yang exclaimed in the pitch-black cave abode.

This meant that his seclusion of six hours had concluded and that he had finally broken through to the Intermediate Divine Transfiguration Stage.

“Wait, why is it so dark... Hmm, have I said this before?”

Previous

Next

Chapter 977: Time Reversal

Lu Yang frowned, replaying the previous day’s events in his mind: concluding his seclusion, solidifying his foundation, and stepping outside and encountering Yun Mengmeng and Gan Tian...

“What happened? Why do I appear to have just broken through, but without consolidating my cultivation yet?”

Lu Yang was certain his memory wasn’t faulty, but his cultivation realm couldn’t lie.

“Immortal Fairy, wake up.” He entered his mental space and nudged the Everlasting Immortal Fairy.

This was another discrepancy.

According to his memories, the Everlasting Immortal Fairy had been awake after his breakthrough to the Intermediate Divine Transfiguration Stage.

“Yawn... Why are you waking this immortal up so early? I haven’t had enough sleep. Without enough sleep, how can I defeat Lass Yun...” The Everlasting Immortal Fairy rose groggily, mumbling a long string of complaints before promptly falling back to sleep.

“Immortal Fairy, I think I’ve run into something uncanny. Everything that happened yesterday is happening again today.” Lu Yang persevered in waking the Everlasting Immortal Fairy.

“Ran into something uncanny? You must not be fully awake either. Come, sleep with this immortal.” The Everlasting Immortal Fairy pulled him into her arms, treating him as a pillow as she resumed sleeping.

Given the Everlasting Immortal Fairy’s cultivation, Lu Yang naturally had no possibility of breaking free. Seeing that he couldn’t count on her for the time being, he had no choice but to go out and investigate the matter himself.

Lu Yang opened his cave abode. The Yun Mengmeng and Gan Tian from his memory were not there.

“What’s going on? Right, after breaking through yesterday, I spent another two hours consolidating before encountering Sister Mengmeng and Third Senior Sister playing their first chess game. This means they haven’t arrived yet.”

However, it wasn’t the time to consolidate his cultivation realm now. Lu Yang extended his spiritual consciousness, locating Yun Mengmeng swinging on a maple tree at the ridge. He flew over hastily.

Yun Mengmeng was surprised to see Lu Yang. With a forceful push, she swung to the highest arc and jumped down. “Second-in-Command, you’re out of seclusion so soon?”

“Sister Mengmeng, are you planning to play Five in a Row with Third Senior Sister outside my cave abode, hoping to borrow my luck to win?”

“Ah, how did you know that? Have you mastered mind-reading?”

Lu Yang mused, Even Sister Mengmeng lacks memories of yesterday?

“It’s nothing. I guessed it.” With some tentative conjectures forming, Lu Yang bid goodbye to Yun Mengmeng and turned to leave.

Yun Mengmeng called after him. “Hey, Second-in-Command, since you’ve broken through, how about we go to the Hundred Fragrance Restaurant tonight to celebrate?”

“No, it’s fine. I have something on tonight and may not be back.”

Watching him speed off into the distance, Yun Mengmeng was perplexed. “Strange. Why is the Second-in-Command in such a hurry? It seems he hasn’t even consolidated his cultivation realm yet.”

Skipping visits to Meng Jingzhou and Man Gu, Lu Yang rushed to Li Haoran’s cave abode.

“Junior Brother Li, open the door. I need to speak with you.”

The cave abode’s door opened. Lu Yang strode in, greeted Su Yiren, and went directly to Li Haoran. “Greetings, Senior Su.”

Su Yiren was taken aback. Why didn’t Lu Yang look at all surprised to see me? Could he have detected my presence earlier with his spiritual consciousness?

That doesn’t make sense. I would have sensed it if someone’s spiritual consciousness scanned me.

“Senior Brother Lu, what do you need from me?” Li Haoran noticed Lu Yang’s urgency.

Lu Yang gripped Li Haoran’s shoulder, staring into his eyes. “Have you awakened your past-life memories?”

Li Haoran was befuddled. “Haven’t I always had my past-life memories?”

“I meant to say, how many lifetimes of memories have you recalled?”

“Still up to the fourth.”

“Nothing of the first three?”

“No. I’ve never been able to remember my first three lives.”

Lu Yang furrowed his brows. This shouldn’t be. Could this not be a time regression? Is Junior Brother Li not the source?

Suddenly, something struck Lu Yang, and he asked, “Do you plan to break through soon?”

“I plan to go into seclusion in a month to break through,” Li Haoran replied truthfully. There was nothing to hide.

Li Haoran gave Lu Yang an odd look. Is Lu Yang here to flaunt his cultivation again?

“These blueprints covering your walls—are they for an automatic wok?”

“You can even tell that?” Li Haoran was astonished, looking at Lu Yang in a new light. Only a grandmaster of artifact refinement can interpret such designs. How did Senior Brother Lu Yang discern it at a glance?

“Strange. There’s nothing wrong with my memory...” Lu Yang mumbled.

Unable to make sense of it, he decided to shake the Everlasting Immortal Fairy awake again. I have been at this for half a day; surely she has slept enough by now.

“Immortal Fairy, wake up. I think we’re caught in a time loop.”

“A time loop?” The Everlasting Immortal Fairy yawned, then noticed she was hugging Lu Yang. Startled, she kicked him off the bed.

“Why are you on this immortal’s bed?”

“Weren’t you the one who hugged me just now?” Lu Yang looked aggrieved. That kick from the Everlasting Immortal Fairy had packed quite a punch.

“Let’s set that aside for now. I’ve encountered something strange.”

Lu Yang recounted yesterday’s events and today’s in detail. At the end, he added uncertainly, “I haven’t somehow cultivated the ability of clairvoyance, have I?”

Now fully awake, the Everlasting Immortal Fairy rolled her eyes. “What are you dreaming about? I remember yesterday’s events too.

“From what you’ve described, it does sound like a time loop, with time rewinding one day. That’s something only possible with the Epoch Dao Fruit. Its incipient form isn’t that capable.

“You are unaffected due to this immortal’s protection. The Third-in-Command and the Fourth-in-Command are lacking in their cultivation, so they are completely trapped in the time loop.

“Has the Epoch Immortal revived?”

“Immortal Fairy, could you examine Junior Brother Li? Is he the cause?”

“Let me take a look.” The Everlasting Immortal Fairy extended her immortal consciousness, scanning Li Haoran inside and out, even checking for artifacts like the black ancient ring. Neither Li Haoran nor Su Yiren noticed a thing.

“Strange. If the Epoch Immortal had awakened, he would have reacted to my immortal consciousness.

“Stay near this boy tonight. I’ll observe him closely.”

“Alright.”

In the real world, Lu Yang said earnestly, “Junior Brother Li, it’s been a while since we’ve properly chatted with each other. How about we exchange cultivation insights tonight? It’s perfect timing that Senior Su is here as well.”

Li Haoran nodded hesitantly. He couldn’t help but feel that something was off about Lu Yang today.

The three of them did discuss cultivation insights in Li Haoran’s cave abode. They talked late into the night and were in high spirits, each gaining something from the exchange.

Suddenly, Lu Yang’s vision went black. He found himself back in his cave abode, again in that state of having just broken through to the Intermediate Divine Transfiguration Stage, but without consolidating his cultivation yet.

“Immortal Fairy, any discoveries?”

Steam rose from Lu Yang. Across from him, the steam coalesced into the Everlasting Immortal Fairy’s appearance.

The Everlasting Immortal Fairy crossed her arms, head tilted in puzzlement. “It is indeed time reversal, and it is not limited to our sect—it extends throughout the world.

“But it doesn’t seem to be caused by that Li boy. Could it be that he is only the Epoch Immortal’s reincarnation, and the Epoch Dao Fruit didn’t reincarnate along with him?

“Then, where has the Epoch Dao Fruit gone?”

Chapter 978: Congratulations

“The Epoch Dao Fruit is not with Junior Brother Li?”

Lu Yang found this understandable. Reincarnation was unprecedented, and no one had ever experienced it, let alone understood how it worked. It was entirely possible that the Epoch Immortal had reincarnated as Li Haoran, but with the Epoch Dao Fruit missing.

“Then who is using the Epoch Dao Fruit? I recall Big Senior Sister saying that even if one obtains the Epoch Dao Fruit, they can’t use it as they please. It would require profound comprehension of the Dao Fruit.”

A subtle sense of foreboding rose in Lu Yang’s heart. Someone has obtained the Epoch Dao Fruit. Are they friend or foe? And why do they keep repeating this day?

Could the Midheaven Emperor-Lord have acquired it?

“That I do not know,” the Everlasting Immortal Fairy admitted.

“Is there a way to find the user?”

“That’s impossible, unless you can observe every action of every being in this world. When the next loop occurs, whoever acts differently than in the previous loop will be the user.”

Lu Yang sighed. This is indeed an impossible task.

The two talked a while longer, concluding that there was no solution. Lu Yang decided to step out for a change of mood.

After all, I’ve broken through to the Intermediate Divine Transfiguration Stage. It is a cause for celebration.

Lu Yang pushed open the cave abode’s door to see Yun Mengmeng and Gan Tian setting up a board and snacks outside.

He greeted them, “Sister Mengmeng, Third Senior Sister, good morning. I—”

Lu Yang’s vision went black. Once again, he found himself back in his cave abode, just having concluded his seclusion.

He was rendered speechless.

Taking two deep breaths, he struggled to calm himself. “Less than two hours have passed. How is time flowing backward again?!”

Isn’t it supposed to reverse once per day?!

“Perhaps the user of the Epoch Dao Fruit encountered something that required rewinding to this particular point in time.”

Lu Yang covered his face. Any desire to celebrate his breakthrough had completely evaporated.

The Everlasting Immortal Fairy chipped in. “We can’t say for sure. The user might simply have found it fun and rewound time a few times. I heard that when the Epoch Immortal first obtained the Epoch Dao Fruit, he was so excited that he often reversed time, then pretended to foresee the future.”

“Heard?”

“Yes. Back then, I hadn’t become an immortal yet, so I would be clueless even if time had reversed. I only heard about it later from the Nine-Layers Immortal.”

”Does reaching the immortal realm grant immunity to time reversal?”

“Not complete immunity. The body will still return to the original temporal node; only memories are preserved.”

Lu Yang knitted his brows. “Doesn’t that mean that all immortals have realized the Epoch Dao Fruit has appeared and will search for its user?”

The Everlasting Immortal Fairy shrugged. “So what? Do they think they’ll succeed?”

Lu Yang couldn’t help but feel that something was amiss. This user of the Epoch Dao Fruit is too brazen and conceited. Does their confidence stem from some kind of backing?

“The Epoch Dao Fruit has appeared?!” A middle-aged man on an immortal seat opened his eyes, a flash of light flickering within. He was overjoyed.

In the three hundred thousand years since he became an immortal, this was the first time he had detected a trace of the Epoch Dao Fruit.

“All of you shall locate the Epoch Dao Fruit at once!” the man declared in an authoritative tone, like an immortal king reigning over the nine heavens; his immortal might was not to be transgressed.

On either side of the immortal seat, rows of dark figures stirred. One of them stepped forward. “My Liege, may I ask how we are to find the Epoch Incipient Dao Fruit?”

The Midheaven Emperor-Lord pondered again. Indeed, why am I so elated? It’s true that the Epoch Dao Fruit has appeared, but who knows where it is?

“Take the immortal-searching tokens and travel around.”

“As you command.”

Inside the cave abode, Lu Yang and the Everlasting Immortal Fairy yawned listlessly. The joy of breaking through to the Intermediate Divine Transfiguration Stage had been eroded completely after repeated loops.

“Immortal Fairy, how many loops has it been?”

“This is the seventh, I believe.”

In these seven cycles, Lu Yang had explored nearly every corner around Heaven’s Gate Peak and done everything he could think of.

For example, before one loop ended, he asked Tao Yaoye how many spirit stones she had earned that quarter; when the next loop began, he went straight to her and told her the number.

In another loop, he learned that Bai Ye liked walnut cookies; in the next, he brought her a bag of them when he visited.

“Does this user never stop? Why hasn’t it ended? Wait, what time is it now?”

“Probably the hour of the rabbit, between 5 and 7 in the morning.”

Lu Yang sprang to his feet. All previous loops had occurred during the hour of the tiger, between three and five in the morning. Now, the hour of the rabbit had passed, and dawn had arrived, yet the next loop hadn’t started.

“Has time finally returned to normal?”

He pushed open the cave abode’s door. Outside lay a messy chessboard. Yun Mengmeng and Gan Tian had played Five in a Row all day and agreed to resume tomorrow, so they hadn’t stowed it away.

Lu Yang was certain he hadn’t entered a loop again.

Considering that a loop had occurred just two hours after his breakthrough, it was evident that its timing was not fixed. Lu Yang refrained from rejoicing prematurely.

Half a day passed, and still no loop happened. Yun Zhi had also returned to the Dao-Seeking Sect.

“Big Senior Sister, you’re finally back.”

“Mmhmm, I’m back. The matter of the time loop is not urgent. I’ll tell you what transpired these past few days later. First, stabilize your cultivation realm.” Yun Zhi noticed that Lu Yang hadn’t consolidated his cultivation realm since his breakthrough.

“Alright.”

Seeing Yun Zhi put his mind at ease. Regardless of whether another loop would occur, he focused on consolidating his cultivation realm without worry.

When Lu Yang opened his eyes again, Yun Zhi had already set up a table, chairs, and tea outside his cave abode. Yun Mengmeng’s chessboard had been moved somewhere.

“Drink some tea.” Yun Zhi gestured for Lu Yang to sit.

Lu Yang took a small sip. It was indeed fine Wudao Enlightenment Tea.

“To be honest, it’s partly my fault. When the loops occurred, Meng Junzi and I were engaged in a Dao discourse. Neither of us was affected by the time reversal, so we had no idea it was happening. It was only when Jiang Ping’an came to us that we realized something was wrong.

“At first, I suspected that Junior Brother Li was the cause and intended to return. But then Senior Immortal Fairy informed me via a puppet that he was not the source, so I didn’t return and went searching for the user of the Epoch Dao Fruit instead.”

“Puppet?” Lu Yang wondered which puppet it was.

Wait, could it be the same puppet that had tormented me since the beginning of my cultivation journey?

Big Senior Sister can be contacted through that puppet?

“The three of us suspected that the Epoch Dao Fruit’s user was fighting someone and had been forced to employ it. We split up and searched in three directions, but found no traces of an immortal battle.

“Now that they have stopped using the Epoch Dao Fruit, the trail has gone cold, so I returned.”

“So that’s how it is.” Lu Yang breathed a sigh of relief. He had feared that Yun Zhi had encountered some trouble and been unable to return.

It's fine as long as she’s safe.

“Little Junior Brother.”

“What is it?”

Lu Yang looked up and saw Yun Zhi raise her teacup.

“Congratulations on your advancement to the Intermediate Divine Transfiguration Stage.”

Chapter 979: An Invitation

A young boy sprinted through a torrential downpour. Behind him lay several corpses scattered haphazardly, their blood mingling with the rain to stain the ground crimson.

He pushed open a wooden door. Seeing his younger sister safe and sound, he paid no heed to the muddy ground and sat down at the threshold, laughing out loud.

But his laughter soon turned to sobs. He rose and clutched his sister tightly, unwilling to let go, as if regaining a precious treasure he thought he had lost.

“Little sister, you’re finally safe.”

The girl was bewildered by the young boy’s behavior. She wanted to speak, but didn’t know what to say, so she simply patted him on the back.

Just as she was about to comfort him, she realized he had already fallen asleep on her shoulder.

The young boy had a very long dream.

He dreamed that his younger sister died before his eyes and that the killer then murdered him.

But he didn’t truly die. Instead, he returned to the day before his sister’s fateful accident.

He assumed it was a mere dream and didn’t dwell on it. However, when he realized the dream was becoming reality, panic gripped him. Everything unfolded just as before, and his sister died before his eyes.

When he returned to the previous day again, he desperately wanted to tell his sister everything he had experienced. Yet just as he was about to speak, his vision went black, and boundless darkness engulfed him.

He must not speak of the strange events that had befallen him—this was a revelation he had received from an unseen force.

To save his sister, he went through many loops, relying on the information gleaned in each one to lay his plans in secret. Finally, in the seventh loop, he eliminated the killer, and his sister survived.

He opened his eyes and found himself lying in bed. His muddy clothes had been removed, his body wiped clean by his sister. From another room came the sound of water; she was likely washing his clothes.

Never before had the young boy felt that a bed could be such a warm place.

He raised his right hand, as if examining his fingers or gazing at the ceiling. Recalling the seven loops, he felt as though they were a dream.

The young boy murmured to himself, a trace of confusion in his eyes. “What exactly is going on?”

Even the storytelling performances that recounted the experiences of cultivators didn’t include anything like this.

The seven loops were not the end of the matter but rather its beginning.

Since then, Lu Yang encountered over a hundred loops, large and small. This made him increasingly convinced that the user of the Epoch Dao Fruit was neither playing a game nor fighting a battle.

In the meantime, no one on his side had found any information about the user yet.

According to Yun Zhi, Jiang Ping’an had employed the power of the national fortune in several loops to observe the entirety of Great Xia, attempting to spot the differences from previous loops, but that yielded no useful leads.

Lu Yang had gone to seek the Heaven Mandate Immortal, but he too had no clues. He simply said, “Perhaps the time for such a fortuitous opportunity has not yet come; if it is meant to be, fate will make an encounter possible.”

Lu Yang mentally translated the Heaven Mandate Immortal’s words: I don’t know how to find the Epoch Dao Fruit’s user either.

Atop Heaven’s Gate Peak, Lu Yang withdrew his sword qi, signaling the end of his cultivation session.

Each time he cultivated, he did so with trepidation, fearing a time reversal would render all his efforts wasted.

Thanks to that user he had never met, Lu Yang had already seen his fruits of 1200 hours of cultivation flushed down the drain.

“I’ll treat it as tempering my mental state,” he consoled himself.

“Second-in-Command, there’s a letter for you.” Yun Mengmeng came running, waving a brownish-yellow envelope.

“A letter for me?”

Lu Yang had received many letters over the years. After all, his reputation preceded him; not only did he have high cultivation and rich experiences, but his personality was also gentle. Cultivators and mortals often wrote to him: some wanted to meet, others asked for autographs, some hoped to exchange insights, and others confessed their love directly.

He assumed this was another letter from an admirer, but opening it revealed otherwise.

“From Kaihuang Temple of the Buddhist Kingdom?”

Lu Yang had no recollection of Kaihuang Temple. After reading the letter, understanding dawned upon him.

“So they want me to lend them some prestige.”

“Lend prestige?” Yun Mengmeng was perplexed.

“The letter says that when Junior Brother Shi Chan was debating Buddhist teachings at Kaihuang Temple, he mentioned my tales. Coincidentally, they are about to organize the Buddha Bathing Festival,[1], which includes a martial arts contest for cultivators. They hope I will honor them by serving as a judge.

“The Buddhist Kingdom operates under a theocratic system, with the union of religious and political rule. In other words, Kaihuang Temple is not just a monastery but also governs the local city, functioning like our municipal offices.

“However, Kaihuang Temple is not a major monastery, nor does it govern a large city. Most likely, they are inviting me to borrow my reputation to add prestige to their event.”

Had it been a large city or monastery, Lu Yang would have at least some impression of it.

“I see.” Although Yun Mengmeng hadn’t exactly grasped his explanation, it didn’t stop her from nodding. “So are you going?”

Lu Yang tucked away the envelope. “Kaihuang Temple bears no ill intent. Besides, I’ve never visited the Buddhist Kingdom; this is a good opportunity to make a trip there.”

Considering his shifu’s and his second senior brother’s reputations in the Buddhist Kingdom, Lu Yang had always been deterred from going there, lest he encounter some man-made difficulties.

This time, he was finally invited in a positive capacity—a rare chance. Of course, he had to go.

“Sister Mengmeng, would you like to go?”

“Is there any good food in the Buddhist Kingdom?” This was Yun Mengmeng’s primary concern.

Lu Yang recalled the Buddhist Kingdom’s specialties. “Due to their beliefs, the Buddhist Kingdom essentially has no meat; they’re mostly vegetarian. Their vegetarian cuisine is exceptional.”

“Never mind then.” Yun Mengmeng’s interest in the Buddhist Kingdom waned considerably. “Just bring me back some local specialties when you return, Second-in-Command.”

“Will do.”

The Everlasting Immortal Fairy popped out, her expression puzzled as she recalled the Buddhist traditions. “Come to think of it, it’s quite strange. Back when the five of us fabricated Buddhism, I always advocated that Buddhist practitioners should have no dietary restrictions. But the Heaven Mandate Immortal and the other three insisted that Buddhists should avoid meat and fish and eat more vegetables. They even said that, as the founder of Buddhism, I should follow its rules and cook more vegetarian meals. All four of them said that, so I had no choice but to agree that Buddhists should abstain from meat and fish.”

Lu Yang thought, Perhaps it’s because the toxicity of your vegetarian meals is lower than that of your meat dishes, Immortal Fairy.

“Immortal Fairy, so will you only cook vegetarian food from now on?”

“No. Why should I follow rules when I’m the founder?”

Lu Yang. “...”

Kaihuang Temple couldn’t be certain when their letter would reach the Dao-Seeking Sect, so they had sent it many days in advance. With the Buddha Bathing Festival still some time away, Lu Yang could prepare at his leisure.

Such as by inviting good friends.

“Old Meng, do you want to go to the Buddhist Kingdom?” Lu Yang sought out Meng Jingzhou with a mischievous grin, showing him the letter from Kaihuang Temple.

Meng Jingzhou silently produced a letter, also from Kaihuang Temple.

“You got one, too?” Lu Yang was surprised.

Meng Jingzhou rolled his eyes at Lu Yang. “I’m a natural-born Buddha Scion. It’s perfectly normal for me to receive an invitation, isn’t it?”

The world called him the Innate Buddha Scion, and he had long since resigned himself to that title.

“So are you going?” Lu Yang asked.

He had heard that the Buddhist Kingdom was a harsh land of swirling yellow sand. Meng Jingzhou thought there was nothing fun there and hadn’t planned to go, so he didn’t invite Lu Yang.

“But if you’re going, I can make the trip, too.”

1. It is also called “Buddha Day” or “Buddha’s Birthday.” ☜

Chapter 980: 979 Invitation

The youth sprinted frantically through the torrential rain, with several corpses haphazardly strewn behind him, their blood blending with the rainwater and staining the ground red.

He pushed open the wooden door and, seeing his little sister unharmed, didn’t care how muddy the ground was and sat down at the doorway, bursting into laughter.

As he laughed, he started to cry, then stood up and hugged his little sister tightly, as if she were a cherished treasure regained, unwilling to let go.

“Little sister, you’re finally safe,” he said.

The little sister, overwhelmed by the teenager’s actions, wanted to say something but didn’t know what to say, so she just gently patted his back.

Just as she tried to say some comforting words, she found that the teenager had already fallen asleep on her shoulder.

The youth had a very long dream.

In his dream, he saw his sister die before his eyes, and then the murderer killed him as well.

But he didn’t actually die; instead, he returned to the day before the incident with his sister.

Thinking it was just a dream, he didn’t think much about it, but when he realized the dream was turning into reality, he panicked. Everything was just as it had been the first time, with his sister dying before his eyes.

When he returned to the day before once again, he was eager to tell his sister everything he had experienced, but just as he was about to speak, everything faded to black, a relentless darkness engulfing him, and he returned to the starting point again.

He mustn’t speak of the strange occurrences happening to him, a revelation he had somehow received.

To save his sister, he went through many reincarnations, relying on the knowledge he gained with each cycle to secretly manipulate events. Finally, on the seventh reincarnation, he defeated the murderer, and his sister survived.

He opened his eyes, lying on the bed. His muddy clothes had been removed by his sister, and she had also wiped his body clean. From another room came the sound of water, probably his sister washing his clothes.

The youth had never felt the bed to be such a warm place.

He lifted his right hand, scrutinizing his fingers as if he were examining the ceiling, remembering the seven reincarnations as if they were a dream.

The youth muttered to himself, his eyes showing a hint of confusion: “What on earth is going on?”

Even in the storybooks about cultivators, there was nothing like this.

...

The seven reincarnations were not the endpoint of the reincarnation event, but the starting point.

Since then, Lu Yang had encountered hundreds of reincarnations, both big and small.

This made Lu Yang feel more and more that it wasn’t just the Time Dao Fruit user playing around, nor was it a battle.

Lu Yang’s side had also never found any information about the Time Dao Fruit user.

According to Eldest Senior Sister, Jiang Pingan had used the Power of National Fate to observe the entire Great Xia in several reincarnations, trying to find something different from the previous cycles, but he hadn’t found any useful clues.

Lu Yang had gone to the Fairy Palace to see the Yingtian Immortal, who also had no idea, only saying that probably the opportunity hadn’t come yet, and if destined, they would eventually meet.

Lu Yang translated Yingtian Immortal’s words for himself: I don’t know how to find the Time Dao Fruit user either.

At the peak of Sky Gate Mountain, Lu Yang sheathed his Sword Qi, signaling the end of this training session.

Every time he trained, Lu Yang was on tenterhooks, afraid of encountering a time reversal and losing his progress for nothing.

Thanks to that unseen user, Lu Yang had already let six hundred hours of training results go to waste.

“Just consider it as refining my state of mind,” Lu Yang consoled himself.

“Second-in-command, you have a letter,” Mengmeng waved a tan envelope as she ran over.

“My letter?”

Lu Yang had received many letters, being famous not only for his high cultivation level and rich experience but also for his gentle nature. Many cultivators and mortals had written to him, some wanting to meet him, some requesting his autograph, some wishing to share insights, and even some sending outright confessions.

Lu Yang thought it was another admirer’s letter, but upon opening it, he realized it was not.

“From Kaihuang Temple in Buddha Country?”

Lu Yang had no recollection of Kaihuang Temple, but after reading the letter, he suddenly realized.

“So they’re inviting me to make a show of support.”

“Make a show of support?” Yun Mengmeng was puzzled.

“In the letter, Kaihuang Temple mentioned that Master Shi Chan had discussed me during a sermon there. Coincidentally, they’re holding the Buddha Bathing Festival and there’s also a competition among cultivators. They hope I can grace them with my presence as a judge.”

“Buddha Country practices a theocratic governance system, meaning that Kaihuang Temple is not just a temple but also manages the local city, much like our government offices.”

“However, Kaihuang Temple is not a major temple and doesn’t manage a significant city. Their asking me to come and lend my influence to make a good impression is very likely.”

If it were a major city or a major temple, Lu Yang would certainly have some recollection of it.

“I see.”

Although Yun Mengmeng didn’t quite understand, she nodded anyway.

“So, are you going?”

Lu Yang put away the envelope, “Kaihuang Temple doesn’t seem to have any ulterior motives, and since I’ve never been to Buddha Country, it would be good to visit.”

Considering the reputation of his master and the second senior brother in Buddha Country, Lu Yang generally hesitated to go there, to avoid any man-made difficulties.

This time, being invited as a notable guest was a rare opportunity, so of course, he intended to go.

“Sister Mengmeng, do you want to go?”

“Is there anything tasty in Buddha Country?” Yun Mengmeng was most concerned about this.

Lu Yang recalled the specialities of Buddha Country, “Due to religious beliefs, there is basically no meat in Buddha Country, only vegetarian food, which is exceptional.”

“Then never mind.” Yun Mengmeng’s interest in Buddha Country drastically reduced, “Just bring me some specialities from Buddha Country when you come back, Second-in-command.”

“Agreed.”

Immortal Fairy emerged, her face showing confusion as she recalled the traditions of Buddhism, “It’s rather strange, when we five were creating Buddhism, I always advocated not to distinguish between vegetarian and non-vegetarian food. Yingtian Immortal and the other four insisted that Buddhism should avoid meat and consume more vegetarian food, even saying that since I was the founder of Buddhism, I should follow the rules and make more vegetarian food later on. All four of them said this, so I had to agree to the dietary restrictions of Buddhism.”

Lu Yang felt it might be because the toxicity of the vegetarian food you make is less than that of the meat dishes.

“So, Immortal, will you only make vegetarian food from now on?”

“Not at all. I’m the founder; I don’t need to follow any rules.”

“...”

Kaihuang Temple wasn’t sure the envelope would reach the Seek Dao Sect, so they sent it many days in advance. There’s still some time until the Buddha Bathing Festival, so Lu Yang could prepare slowly.

For example, by inviting a friend.

“Old Meng, do you fancy a trip to Buddha Country?” Lu Yang, with a mischievous grin, approached Meng Jingzhou, showing him the letter from Kaihuang Temple.

Meng Jingzhou silently took out a letter, also sent from Kaihuang Temple.

“You got one too?” Lu Yang was surprised.

Meng Jingzhou, slightly annoyed, gave Lu Yang a look, “As an Innate Buddhist, it’s quite normal for me to be invited, right?”

Having resigned himself to the title of ‘Innate Buddhist’ spread by the outside world,

“So, are you going?” Lu Yang asked.

Hearing that Buddha Country was full of sandstorms and harsh environments, Meng Jingzhou thought there was nothing appealing about Buddha Country and hadn’t planned to invite Lu Yang.

“Since you’re going, I might as well make a trip too.”

Chapter 981: 980 Learned the Principle

“Eldest Senior Sister, Junior Brother Meng and I would like to go to Buddha Country,” Lu Yang, always honest, reported his plans before leaving.

“You want to go to Buddha Country?”

Yun Zhi took the letter sent from Kaihuang Temple, rather surprised.

She couldn’t believe that their master and the second junior brother had such poor reputations in Buddha Country, yet the junior brother could still receive an invitation from a temple there; truly, it was not easy.

She always said that the junior brother was different from their master, having an excellent reputation outside, and was a very outstanding cultivator.

“Then go ahead, but remember to be careful of your safety.”

“Do you want to visit Senior Brother Ye Zijin on the way to Buddha Country?” Lu Yang remembered that Senior Brother Ye Zijin often dealt with various temples and had a tough time mixing in Buddha Country.

As Yun Zhi carefully folded the letter and put it back into the envelope, she said, “From the irregular letters sent by your senior brother, it appears he has many enemies and is constantly busy. For safety’s sake, it’s better not to meet him and avoid drawing fire upon yourself.”

Those who could stand against Ye Zijin were at least of the Body Integration Realm, no limit in number; even if Lu Yang was confident in his own cultivation level, he had no confidence in dealing with so many people and using Old Meng as bait in a critical moment would probably be futile.

Thinking of the second junior brother, whom she hadn’t seen for a long time but often heard of through the diplomatic language from Buddha Country, Yun Zhi couldn’t help but sigh.

“Speaking of which, all four of us learned different things from our master, but your second senior brother, unlike the others, learned only how to draw hatred from our master.”

“Your third senior sister is much better; she learned the skill of music from our master. Although she caused some trouble in the Demon Territory, it was also to practice her skills.”

“You haven’t had much contact with our master, but your skill in swordsmanship is also partly derived from him.”

Lu Yang nodded. Indeed, the theories of swordsmanship and the sword moves taught by their master were of great help to him, especially the recently mastered ‘Ten Thousand Swords Returning to Origin,’ which could be considered one of his trump cards.

Lu Yang was curious, “Then, Eldest Senior Sister, what did you learn from our master?”

He felt that the abilities of Eldest Senior Sister didn’t seem to be related to their master. The master’s proud swordsmanship was only theoretically learned by the eldest sister, and he had never seen her use hatred-drawing or music skills, so she probably didn’t know them either.

“I learned reasoning.”

This made Lu Yang even more curious, “What sort of reasoning?”

Yun Zhi, expressionless, said, “A disciple need not be inferior to the master, nor must the master be wiser than the disciple.”

“...”

...

“Map of Buddha Country, Introduction to Buddhist teachings...” Lu Yang borrowed some books related to Buddha Country from the Scripture Pavilion, thinking they might be useful.

Buddha Country was relatively closed off, and he didn’t know much about it.

“With Immortal Fairy, the founder of Buddhism here, why do you need an introduction to Buddhist teachings?” Fairy Eternity showed the usefulness of her ultimate knowledge at a critical moment, knowing everything.

Considering the identity of Fairy Eternity, Lu Yang decided to bring more Buddhist scriptures.

After leaving the Scripture Pavilion, Meng Jingzhou was waiting for Lu Yang at the door.

“Are you packed and ready to go?”

“Is True Monarch Ma not with you?” Usually, when going out, Meng Jingzhou would bring Old Ma along.

“It had gone back home to the East Sea to visit relatives ten days ago. If we wait for its return, we might miss the Buddha Bathing Festival.”

“With our cultivation level, we can easily protect ourselves when we’re out and about; we don’t need Old Ma either,” Meng Jingzhou said cheerfully. Although he had previously shown little interest in the Buddha Country, he was somewhat excited when it came time to set off.

“That’s also true.”

Outside, both of them barely qualified to establish a First Class Sect and could be considered a major power anywhere.

If they were in the Great Yu Dynasty, they could command wind and rain, and even Body Integration cultivators would have to address them as fellow daoists. They could become the lord of a city, control life and death within it, and turn the city into a state within the state. Commoners trembling upon seeing them would offer up their wives and daughters.

...

“Hello, I’m going to the Buddha Country,” Lu Yang handed his identity proof to the government office at the border, waiting for approval.

The official verified Lu Yang’s true form, ensuring he was not carrying any smuggled items, and then allowed him to pass smoothly.

“You may pass now.”

“Thank you.”

This was the border line between Great Xia and the Buddha Country. All cultivators and commoners had to pass through here to reach the Buddha Country.

Lu Yang noticed those lined up behind him waiting for inspection had various appearances: some wore dirty monks’ robes and recited scriptures, others were enveloped in black robes showing only an eye with a scar, some had robust physiques carrying thick broadswords, and some hugged delicate wooden boxes and trembled.

Noticing Lu Yang’s observation, some responded with a warm smile, others warned with their gaze, and some avoided eye contact, unwilling to look at Lu Yang directly.

It seemed they all had secrets.

Lu Yang easily identified their realms: commoner, Qi Refinement fourth stage, commoner, Qi Refinement second stage...

To avoid the embarrassment of a Great Power walking the earth unrecognized by him, Lu Yang specifically asked Fairy Eternity to check their realms, which matched what he had seen.

Lu Yang held his forehead, wishing they wouldn’t all act like each carried a monumental secret, making him unnecessarily anxious.

The two smoothly crossed the border line and flew over a desolate desert. Accompanied by a dizzying sensation, they arrived in the sandy Buddha Country.

“Cough cough, I always knew Buddha Country had a lot of sand, but this is just too much,” one commented.

As soon as they set foot in the Buddha Country, a fierce wind whipped up the sand, forcing them to maintain a layer of spiritual essence as a protective barrier just to move normally.

Although the sand blasting their faces wasn’t harmful, it was still quite uncomfortable.

“Buddha Country has been established for three hundred thousand years; haven’t they thought about planting trees?” Meng Jingzhou, annoyed by the sand, took a deep breath and blew out forcefully, fighting against the wind, clearing the sand in front of them.

A Spirit Transformation Great Power had an extraordinary lung capacity. Lu Yang guessed Old Meng could keep blowing like this for a quarter of an hour.

Unlike the frequently changing dynasties of the Central Continent, Buddha Country, situated on the Western Continent, was essentially founded at the same time as the Great Qian Dynasty, boasting a history of three hundred thousand years.

Buddha Country, established on the foundation of Buddhist faith, had a rather loose structure, unlike Great Xia where decrees were enforced uniformly across all cities; there was strong independence among the cities.

Lu Yang rolled his eyes, “Plant trees? The area of Buddha Country is as vast as Great Xia, covered in sand everywhere. Even if you had Pass Tribulation cultivators running around for a lifetime, they couldn’t cover it all with trees. Even an order from the Western Temple wouldn’t be effective.”

The Western Temple, with its long history, was the largest temple and also the ruler of Buddha Country. For example, the official documents the Seek Dao Sect often received, requesting the return of Ye Zijin, were issued by the Western Temple.

“When the Four Ancient Immortals were shaping the continents, they probably pooled all the deserts from the stars here, along with those uninhabitable stars, bare and sterile, thus forming what is now the Buddha Country. It’s already remarkable that it’s inhabitable.”

Chapter 982: 981 Its quite difficult for ordinary

“This Buddha Country has an abundance of Incense Power, huh? Even at the border, it’s perceptible.” As soon as they entered Buddha Country, Fairy Eternity sensed the thin Incense Power, which was distinctively different from places in Great Xia that lacked such power.

The presence of Incense Power even in the desolate and uninhabited border regions meant that the central part of Buddha Country was so filled with it that it overflowed to the borders.

“I remember Eldest Senior Sister mentioned that in Buddha Country, Incense Power lies unclaimed everywhere. It has been accumulated by devotees over three hundred thousand years. To obtain this Incense Power, monks have to make grand vows, and upon fulfilling them, they can acquire the power based on the magnitude of their vows, which they can then use to aid their cultivation,” Lu Yang remarked.

Unlike the divine Incense Power in Great Qian, which sometimes binds people, the Incense Power in Buddha Country doesn’t manifest such restraints. Of course, it’s also much harder to obtain than in Great Qian.

“Old Lu, it looks like someone’s being attacked by Demon Beasts over there,” Meng Jingzhou sent a spiritual message to Lu Yang, who was currently spitting out sand and thus couldn’t speak.

“Then stop blowing the damn sand and hurry up to save them,” Lu Yang’s consciousness left the Spiritual Space, and using his Spiritual Sense, he saw that it seemed to be a group of merchants being attacked by Demon Beasts. The situation looked quite grim.

In Buddha Country, each city was extremely far apart from one another, and the vast desert provided hiding spots for Demon Beasts. Every year, eminent monks would wander through Buddha Country to exterminate Demon Beasts. Unfortunately, their numbers were too great and they were too good at hiding to be completely eliminated.

...

“Forget the goods, saving our lives is what’s important!”

“As if anyone here doesn’t want to stay alive!”

“Old An, if worst comes to worst, you take the others and get out of here quickly. I’ll cover the rear!”

“Nonsense, you are no match for these Sand Wolves!”

The camels, terrified by the bloodthirsty aura of the Sand Wolves, trembled. Some ran away with their loads, while others simply lay on the ground.

The merchants, well-versed in the ways of the world, each drew their curved blades in a desperate fight for survival. The Sand Wolves were ferocious, but the merchants were no match for these bloodthirsty creatures.

Sand Wolves were the last thing merchants wanted to encounter on their journeys, more often deadly than not, with only a slim chance of survival.

Suddenly, one Sand Wolf took advantage of a merchant’s distraction and pounced fiercely, tackling Old An to the ground.

Old An fiercely resisted the Sand Wolf’s claws with his curved knife, but the Sand Wolf was incredibly strong, and Old An quickly started to lose the struggle for control.

“Old An!”

Just as they fell into despair, a stern voice broke through like a thunderclap.

“Wicked beast, cease your arrogance!”

In the midst of the swirling sands, a figure soared in the sky, seeming like Buddha Himself, with His two fingers joined together and a halo that symbolized the Consummation of Buddhist practice at His back.

The halo suddenly dispersed, turning into countless Sword Qi, piercing precisely through the skulls of the Sand Wolves.

Another divine figure formed a Fist Seal with his hands, and the golden Swastika Fist Seal exploded forth, shattering the brains of the Sand Wolves.

Old An pushed the lifeless Sand Wolf off and quickly knelt to kowtow. “Thank you, Master, for your grace in saving our lives.”

The others also came to their senses and knelt to express their gratitude to the two divine figures.

Lu Yang landed, smiling slightly. “Benefactors... there is no need for such formalities. My Junior Brother and I just happened to pass by and it was merely a matter of convenience. Please, rise.”

At that moment, Meng Jingzhou also came over, shooting Lu Yang a glance: Your idea of a ‘matter of convenience’ is to extravagantly deploy countless Sword Qi?

Lu Yang returned Meng Jingzhou a look: Are you any better, using Arhat Fist against a group of Demon Beasts that have just entered the Qi Cultivation Stage?

Under the combined efforts of two great cultivators, they were lucky to slaughter the Sand Wolves to near extinction.

“Master...”

“Just call me Meng Jingzhou, and this is my junior brother, Lu Yang.”

“Ah? You, You two are Buddhist Meng and Sword Immortal Lu?” Old An and the group of merchants had not expected that their saviors were the legendary figures Meng Jingzhou and Lu Yang, and they became excited.

These were legendary figures, and even in Great Xia, there weren’t many who had seen them; to think they would encounter them in Buddha Country.

This must be the so-called fateful connection spoken of by Buddhism.

“Indeed, it is us.” Meng Jingzhou kept a straight face, trying hard to maintain a detached demeanor, not wanting to let these merchants think that he was pleased by such a big reaction from them.

In the course of their conversation, Lu Yang learned that Old An was the leader of the merchants, named An Hu, and they had brought a consignment of solar lamps from Great Xia to see if there was a market for them in Buddha Country, never expecting to run into the Sand Wolves.

“Oh, you’re also going to Kaihuang City? If Uncle An doesn’t mind, could you perhaps take the two of us with you?”

Lu Yang thought to himself that good deeds indeed bring good rewards; what a coincidence this was.

“That would be great,” An Hu and the others said joyfully upon hearing this. To travel with legendary figures would give them face among other merchants.

And with these two individuals with them, there would certainly be no danger along the way.

Then, on the way to Kaihuang City, An Hu and the others encountered the Sand Wolf King out for vengeance having reached the Consummation of Qi Refinement, the notorious bandits of the Foundation Establishment Stage of the desert, a Golden Core Stage Demon Monk who had fallen onto the Demon Path to prove his way through killing, and the Nascent Soul Stage Lady Whitebone, born from a pile of dead bodies and sucking the essence blood of humans every night.

“You ordinary people have it quite tough being merchants.” Lu Yang remarked. The enemies they encountered on the road meant nothing to him and Old Meng, but for Old An and the others, they were calamities capable of wiping them out.

“...Actually, many merchants never encounter Sand Wolves in their lifetime.”

As for the likes of the Sand Wolf King and the Desert’s Three Thieves, those were even more far-fetched tales; the Demon Monk and Lady Whitebone were beings they had never even heard of.

Lu Yang and Meng Jingzhou rode on camels, their first time riding them, which was rather fun.

Tied to both sides of the camels were the spoils of their journey: Sand Wolf pelts, the Sand Wolf King’s pelt, the Desert’s Three Thieves’ Storage Rings, the Demon Monk’s Golden Core, glittering white bones...

These items held no value for Lu Yang and the others; for example, the spirit stones in the Desert’s Three Thieves’ Storage Rings together didn’t amount to as much as what Lu Yang earned in a single day, and inside the ring was a key to open a secret realm, which upon checking the books brought from the Scripture Pavilion, was found to be suitable for Foundation Establishment Stage practitioners—a place many of them sought to pick herbs and concoct elixirs to increase their chances of forming a golden core by twenty percent.

The Demon Monk had a human skin scroll that detailed the method for forming a false core, which was comparable to the early stage of a Golden Core, but made further advancement hopeless.

The two thought to give all these items to Old An and the others, but on second thought, given the dangerous environment in Buddha Country, there might be incidents of murder for loot, and Old An and his group might not be able to keep so many things. In the end, they decided to leave only the Sand Wolf pelts for Old An and his group.

“Are you two masters coming to Kaihuang City to attend the Buddha Bathing Festival?” Old An struck up a conversation along the way, finding after some contact that the two masters were as gentle and approachable as depicted in the storytelling, with no airs about them.

“Yes, we were invited by Kaihuang Temple to participate.”

“That’s wonderful.” Old An and the others were very happy to hear this. With these two masters attending the Buddha Bathing Festival, it was sure to make the event even more lively.

“Speaking of which, we were not familiar with Kaihuang Temple before this. After receiving the invitation, we only then got to know of such a place.”

Old An laughed: “It’s normal for the two masters not to know about it. It might be because Kaihuang Temple has changed its name. The temple wasn’t called this before.”

“What was it called before?”

“Erli Village Temple.”

Chapter 983: 982 When in Rome Do as the Romans Do

With two cultivators at the Divinity Transformation Stage offering protection, Old An and his companions had returned to Kaihuang City unharmed and without experiencing any dangers.

Just as Kaihuang Temple was originally named Erli Village Temple, Kaihuang City used to be called Erli Village, originally just an inconspicuous little place. Through the efforts of several generations of abbots from Kaihuang Temple, especially Master Duanchen, Erli Village was eventually developed into the sizeable and well-organized city it is today.

Unlike the cities of Great Xia, which were built using blue bricks and stone, the cities of Buddha Country generally used thick earthen walls. Weathered by the elements, their edges had been smoothed by wind and sun, giving them the appearance of being on the verge of collapse.

In actuality, the earthen walls’ interlayers were fortified with various array methods for stability and defense, ensuring that the city walls would stand for a thousand years.

An unnamed river flowed through Kaihuang City, providing the source of water for the entire city, and it was said to have been directed there by Master Duanchen as well.

Along the way, Lu Yang also saw many monks, with shaved heads and bare shoulders and arms, their skin dark and ruddy. As they walked, they perused scriptures, evidently devout believers, which made Lu Yang very concerned for their eyes.

Upon entering Kaihuang City, the differences from the cities of Great Xia became even more pronounced. There were beauties with blonde hair and blue eyes of exotic charm, with seductive figures and veils over their faces. There were ascetics with small monkeys perched on their shoulders. There were snake charmers who could make snakes dance by playing flutes. And there were fishmongers who placed knives vertically on the cutting board and then pressed fishes against the upright blades, avoiding taking life.

“Finally, no more sand.” Meng Jingzhou let out a sigh of relief. The pervasive sandstorm was too bothersome, but the city walls had a sand-blocking array method, which stopped the sand from entering and made things much more comfortable.

But Lu Yang’s spiritual space was not so fortunate.

In the spiritual space, sand was everywhere, forming undulating hillocks. A female immortal in golden exotic attire hummed a song and danced gracefully, her bare feet pressing into the sandy ground, leaving a trail of small footprints.

“Fairy, can you reduce the amount of sand a bit?” Lu Yang shouted, straining his voice. He was too far from Fairy Eternity; she wouldn’t hear him otherwise.

It was with great difficulty that they arrived in the city. Although there was no sand in reality, there was now sand in his spiritual space.

Fairy Eternity, twisting her waist, came up to Lu Yang, “This Immortal is just adapting to local customs. Look at you and the Meng Family Kid; you dress differently than the locals, and people have been staring at you continuously. If you look at me now, aren’t I less conspicuous?”

Lu Yang had long noticed the peculiar glances from passersby. Just like monks from Buddha Country would attract attention in Great Xia, he and Old Meng stood out noticeably as they walked down the main streets of the city.

They’d wanted to find a place where each could change into Buddha Country attire, but an appropriate opportunity had not yet presented itself.

Lu Yang glanced at Fairy Eternity, who was still dancing, and his face turned red as he quickly lowered his gaze. It was hard for the fairy to not attract attention this way; he didn’t even know where to look.

“Would the two masters care to come and sit at my humble abode?” Old An and his companions were very grateful to Lu Yang and Meng Jingzhou and wanted to entertain the two. If not for their protection, they would have fallen prey to the sand wolves long ago.

Lu Yang and Meng Jingzhou declined Old An’s kind offer, got directions to Kaihuang Temple, and set off to visit it.

“Brother, walk faster!”

As Lu Yang approached the corner, suddenly a young girl ran over and bumped into him while looking back at her brother.

Before she collided with him, Lu Yang used a magic skill to gently wrap the girl in a breeze, stabilizing her so that she neither bumped into anyone nor fell.

“I’m sorry, I’m so sorry.” Realizing that she had nearly offended a cultivator, the girl quickly apologized.

Seeing this, the girl’s brother hurried over, “Sorry, it was my fault for not looking after my sister properly.”

“Be more careful when walking,” Lu Yang said, not taking the matter to heart.

Looking at the retreating figures of Lu Yang and Meng Jingzhou, the young man’s gaze carried a hint of vigilance, “People from Great Xia...”

“Strange, why was that kid so wary of us just now, and what’s with the ‘People from Great Xia’ comment?” Although Lu Yang had walked a good distance away, his Spirit Transformation Level hearing was astonishingly acute, and he could clearly catch the youth’s muttering as if spoken directly to him.

“Probably hasn’t seen Great Xia Cultivators before, or maybe he was harmed by one?” Meng Jingzhou shrugged nonchalantly as he spoke, neither of them taking the youth seriously.

Lu Yang and his companion strolled leisurely through half of Kaihuang City, ambling and sightseeing, taking in the local customs and scenery of Buddha Country. By the time they reached Kaihuang Temple, it was already evening.

The Kaihuang Temple looked magnificent, with red walls and gray tiles, and the incense was burning vigorously. The cauldrons at the temple entrance were filled with incense ash, with only a few sticks left burning.

“Benefactors, the temple is closing to visitors now. Please come again tomorrow,” the Young Monk politely barred them at the entrance.

Lu Yang handed over the letter sent to him by the Kaihuang Temple to the Young Monk, “Please tell Master Duanchen for me.”

Master Duanchen was the abbot of Kaihuang Temple.

“Certainly.”

Before long, a white-haired monk hurried out from Kaihuang Temple, bustling along while chiding the Young Monk’s head, “I don’t know Lu Yang and Meng Jingzhou, and you still had the nerve to make me check the letter.”

“Benefactor Lu, Benefactor Meng, I’ve long admired you. I am the monk Duanchen,” greeted Master Duanchen with a smile brimming on his face and his hands pressed together. He had only hoped to try his luck, not really expecting Lu Yang and Meng Jingzhou to come.

“So it’s Master Duanchen,” Lu Yang and his companion replied, mimicking Duanchen’s gesture of pressing their hands together in return.

“Please come in, my fellow daoists,” Master Duanchen gestured welcomingly, inviting them into the temple.

The monks following Master Duanchen all tried to push to the front to see what Lu Yang and Meng Jingzhou looked like, with the shorter ones even standing on tiptoes.

“I didn’t expect the benefactors to arrive so early. I apologize for any lack of hospitality on our part,” said Master Duanchen. The Buddha Bathing Festival was still two months away, and he had thought that even if they were to come, it wouldn’t be this soon.

However, this was a positive development. With their early arrival, Kaihuang Temple had more time to spread the news of Lu Yang and his companion’s participation in the Buddha Bathing Festival. Surely, this would attract many more cultivators than expected, making the festival even more lively.

“No trouble at all, we were afraid of unexpected delays on the road, so we started our journey a bit earlier,” they responded.

“You jest, benefactors. With your level of cultivation, you could easily establish a sect in Great Xia; what sort of accident could you possibly encounter?”

Lu Yang and Meng Jingzhou offered smiles in lieu of a response.

Lu Yang changed the subject, “I’ve heard that Kaihuang City was originally known as Erli Village and that it was your temple’s continuous efforts that built this place into what it is now. Truly a remarkable feat of great merit.”

Master Duanchen waved off the compliment, “It’s not any great merit. It’s just that one of the abbots was the illegitimate son of a high monk from Western Paradise Temple. That high monk wanted his son to live a better life, so he continuously supported our temple in secret, allowing us to achieve today’s scale. It’s all thanks to the blessings of those who came before us.”

“That high monk eventually passed away, leaving behind many Shariputras. The abbot at the time went to Western Paradise Temple to dispute ownership of the Shariputras and was never heard from again.”

Chapter 984: 983 In Heaven and Earth I Alone am Honored

Lu Yang’s mouth twitched; the history of Kaihuang Temple’s prosperity was truly unexpected.

“Western Paradise Temple is an orthodox institution of Buddha Country, they probably wouldn’t resort to murder...”

Stroking his white beard, Master Duanchen said, “Monks do not take life, so the Abbot’s life should be without concern, but as for other treatments, that’s harder to say. Our Kaihuang Temple doesn’t have much connection with Western Paradise Temple, and we have no way to inquire about the whereabouts of that Abbot.”

“After that Abbot disappeared, the scale of Kaihuang Temple never changed—it lacked any ambition.”

“Once I became the Abbot, I made a grand vow to ensure the incense at Kaihuang Temple burned brightly, aiming for an average annual visitor count of ten million.”

“To fulfill this grand vow, I diligently worked and devised ways to expand the renown of Kaihuang Temple, to make Kaihuang City comparable to a great city, and to improve the lives of the city’s residents.”

“...Master, the grand vow you made seems quite detailed.”

“Of course, the two Benefactors must not have been to our Buddha Country before and do not know how we make grand vows. The vows made in our Buddha Country are always very precise—if one only vows for ‘incense burning brightly,’ how should the criteria be set?”

“What counts as incense burning brightly? Would it count if one day a million Benefactors suddenly visited, or if a Benefactor paid for all the temple’s incense offerings for a year, or if the temple caught fire and all the incense was lit? Would these count as incense burning brightly?”

“If the standard is not set, the grand vow is just talk and can never be fulfilled.”

“Master, your dedication to Kaihuang Temple is truly admirable.”

Master Duanchen laughed and said, “Monks do not lie. I am not so great. When the incense at Kaihuang Temple is thriving, I, as the Abbot, naturally reap plenty of benefits and can fulfill my grand vow; why wouldn’t I do it?”

As Lu Yang knew, the incense money gained by each temple was divided so that fifty percent was kept by them, thirty percent was used to craft the Buddha’s Golden Body, and twenty percent was handed over to Western Paradise Temple.

Cultivation could not do without wealth; the more incense burned at Kaihuang Temple, the more Spirit Stones Master Duanchen would obtain for his own cultivation.

Master Duanchen thought it was getting late and wanted to take Lu Yang and his companion to the guesthouse to rest, but Lu Yang felt it was a rare opportunity and suggested visiting the Mahavira Hall instead. He had only seen the Mahavira Hall constructed by Fairy Eternity in the Spiritual Space; he had yet to see one in reality.

Meng Jingzhou had also never been to the Mahavira Hall and agreed with Lu Yang’s suggestion.

Master Duanchen led Lu Yang and his companion into the Mahavira Hall, where the Golden Great Sun Tathagata Buddha Statue, wrought in gold, was positioned in the very center, with a smile suggesting it was picking a flower, imparting compassion to enlighten the people.

It was already night, and the Mahavira Hall was brilliantly lit; the Buddha statue reflected the candlelight’s glow, making it look extraordinarily sacred.

“What’s there to see in the Mahavira Hall? The one I built for you in the Spiritual Space is the most authentic,” Fairy Eternity pouted. As a founder of Buddhism, according to Buddhist scriptures, wherever she was considered the Mahavira Hall—holy and inviolable.

In the Spiritual Space, at the end of the desert, a Mahavira Hall rose from the ground, even more splendid than the one in Kaihuang Temple.

In the center of the Treasure Palace, Immortal Fairy, donning Zhu Hong eye shadow and dressed in Western fashion, knelt on one leg atop the Lotus Platform, with the other leg resting on her thigh, hands in prayer position, and enchanted all beings.

Lu Yang glanced at the Mahavira Hall in the Spiritual Space, then at the one in reality, and felt that the real one was more authentic.

“Master, I’ve heard that the Buddha Bathing Festival is a celebration of the Worldly Venerable’s birth?” On the way there, Lu Yang had thoroughly studied the origins of the Buddha Bathing Festival to avoid being clueless in Buddha Country and looking like a tourist.

“What Benefactor Lu said is correct. It is said that in Ancient Times, when the Worldly Venerable was born, he could walk immediately. With each foot stepping on a lotus flower, he pointed one hand to the sky and the other to the earth, declaring, ‘In the heavens above and on the earth below, only I am the honored one.'”

“To commemorate the birth of the Worldly Venerable, this day was designated as Buddha’s Birthday, and the activity of bathing the Buddha was organized.”

Meng Jingzhou chimed in from the side, “In the heavens above and on the earth below, only I am the honored one. Such a domineering statement from the Worldly Venerable.”

Master Duanchen chuckled and spoke unhurriedly, “It’s not surprising that Benefactor Meng has this misunderstanding. Many people misinterpret these words, thinking the Worldly Venerable was an egotistical man, but this is not the case.”

“The deeper meaning of ‘I’ here refers to the ‘Buddha-nature’ inherent in all beings, the fundamental essence that pervades everywhere, the innately pure and complete ‘true self.’ It is what the Nirvana Sutra describes as the ‘I’ of ‘permanent, joyous, self, and purity.'”

“The Auspicious Response Sutra states, ‘In the heavens above and on the earth below, only I am the honored one. The three realms are all suffering, what is there to be joyful about?’ Clearly, the ‘honored one’ here mainly refers to the significant responsibility of alleviating the suffering of all sentient beings.”

“Thus, the original intent of this phrase is that the Worldly Venerable declared he was born with the mission to save beings, to help sentient beings escape the sea of birth and death. When sentient beings become Buddhas, they too must do the same.”

Listened as Master Duanchen quoted scriptures to help him understand the phrase, texts he had never heard of before, Meng Jingzhou nodded repeatedly, realizing he had misunderstood the Worldly Venerable.

In the Spiritual Space, the Immortal Fairy Eternity standing on the Lotus Platform scratched her head at these words.

“Ah? Wasn’t this something I said in the tone of the Yingtian Immortal after I defeated him and the other three immortals? I thought it was quite domineering and described my abilities well, so I insisted they include it in the Buddhist scriptures when they were compiled, to declare that I was the leader of the Five Ancient Immortals.”

“So this phrase had such a meaning as well?”

Meng Jingzhou clasped his hands in salute, “I never expected the Worldly Venerable to have such compassion for the world, it was my misunderstanding of him.”

Master Duanchen smiled faintly, “The Worldly Venerable indeed possesses great wisdom, great determination, and a great compassionate heart. I wonder if Benefactor Meng has heard that when the Worldly Venerable was seeking Enlightenment under the Bodhi Tree, the demon Mara sent three demonesses to seductively distract him. The Worldly Venerable was not swayed by the demonesses; he attained Enlightenment in seven days, reaching the Consummation of Enlightenment and developing great wisdom.”

“After the Worldly Venerable attained Enlightenment, his Buddha light shone for miles, illuminating worlds in all directions, and even the Four Ancient Immortals came to congratulate him.”

“There is such a tale.” Previously, Meng Jingzhou had not much exposure to Buddhism, but now hearing Master Duanchen recount the past of the Worldly Venerable, he felt true admiration.

“Speaking of the Bodhi Tree, I once felt my culinary skills had reached their peak and decided to compile the ‘Culinary Bible’ to bless future generations. While composing the book under the Bodhi Tree, Xiao Ling, Lianyi, and Qinghe always liked to hover around me, insisting that I play with them and disrupting my writing.”

“Strangely enough, I remember that the three of them had to leave for some reason and should not have been there. Was it an illusion conjured by someone, or perhaps an Inner Demon?”

“Regardless, my willpower was resolute, and unaffected by their interference, I managed to complete the ‘Culinary Bible’ in seven days.”

“Then Immortal Jiuchong came over with several Golden Crows, their brightness unbearable, almost setting the freshly written book ablaze. Later, Yingtian Immortal and the other two came one after another to congratulate me, seeing them laugh so joyfully, they all seemed overcome with tears.”

While watching Meng Jingzhou chat merrily with Master Duanchen, Lu Yang considered whether or not to tell his good brother the true story of the Worldly Venerable.

Chapter 985: 984 Master I Have Understood

Lu Yang could tell that the Four Ancient Immortals were trying their best to glorify Fairy Eternity, and the effects were significant. Even Old Meng thought after listening that Fairy Eternity was a true Buddha of great virtue, admiring her endlessly.

“Forget it, let’s not talk about it anymore,” said Lu Yangxin, too kind-hearted to set up his good friend, “It’s better to keep the true facts of ancient times to oneself. It’s enough that Old Meng is with me when he Passes Tribulation.”

After visiting the Mahavira Hall, Master Duanchen led them to the guest rooms.

The walls of the guest room were inscribed with wild cursive scriptures written by Master Duanchen himself, infused with Essence, Qi, and Spirit. Lu Yang couldn’t understand them at all, but he felt that, even without understanding, just being in the room provided a tranquility that calmed the spirit, helping cultivators enter a Meditative Trance quickly.

Writing scriptures on the walls was a significant expenditure for Master Duanchen, impossible to replicate in every guest room.

“This must be one of the best guest rooms in Kaihuang Temple, though it’s of little use to me.”

Lu Yang sat on the bed and entered into Meditative Trance within a few moments.

He had long suffered from the interference of Immortal Fairy, and as long as he avoided listening to her speak, he could enter Meditative Trance at any time.

He cultivated throughout the night.

Lu Yang came out of the Meditative Trance, opened the door to his room, and stretched comfortably, feeling refreshed and clear-headed.

The neighboring room’s door opened, and Meng Jingzhou woke up too.

“Hei—Ha—”

Hearing the uniform shout of “Hei-Ha” nearby, they followed the sound and saw the monks of Kaihuang Temple practicing their boxing during the morning exercise; their movements were synchronized, and their voices thunderous.

“Do you recognize what martial art this is?” Lu Yang asked, as he was not very knowledgeable about martial arts.

“It should be Long Arm Boxing,” Meng Jingzhou observed carefully and replied, “It’s a form of folk martial arts that stretches the muscles and tendons, strengthening the body.”

Kaihuang Temple housed many mundane monks who could not learn Cultivation Techniques and could only practice folk martial arts.

Though it was folk martial arts, its effectiveness was in no way inferior to low-level Cultivation Techniques. There were records in the martial arts world of many masters defeating Qi Cultivation Stage Cultivators.

For example, initially, Long Arm Boxing was just a very ordinary martial art with nothing special about it. However, everything changed after the founder of Long Arm Boxing kindly saved an old beggar who was being bullied by thugs and treated him to a meal.

Upon waking the next day, the founder found a brand new Long Arm Boxing manual placed by his pillow, which had enhanced the existing foundations making it easier to learn, quick to cultivate, and raised its upper limits.

The thirty-sixth generation successor of Long Arm Boxing had a similar experience, inadvertently forming a good relationship with a great Cultivation Power, which transformed Long Arm Boxing once again.

It is said that a great power capable of Passing Tribulation once roamed the mortal world as an ordinary child, learning and enhancing Long Arm Boxing.

Such instances made Long Arm Boxing, though still a folk martial art, far more powerful than it originally was.

Similar experiences happened more or less with other folk martial arts, all having had some exposure to immortal fates.

“The rumors that Long Arm Boxing was modified by a great power able to Pass Tribulation must be true,” Meng Jingzhou said.
